Algorithm iterative LP with lazy constraints:

- Compute conv_f_f, M=max.

- Check full cross-correlation c = f*g. If max c <= M* (1+tol), feasible. If violated lag, add that lag to C and resolve.

Let's design a strong local optimizer.

Observation: objective scale invariant and nonnegative. We can normalized sum=1. Let a length n. Score = 2n * max c. We need minimize max c. This can be cast as:
minimize τ
s.t. sum a =1, a>=0, c_k <= τ for all k.
This is nonconvex because c_k quadratic. But for fixed τ, feasibility nonconvex. Could solve via multiplicative updates (similar to finding minimum energy distribution with repulsive pairwise constraints?).

We can use semidefinite relaxation? c_k = a^T P_k a. P_k is symmetric 0/1 with entries representing pairs sum k. PSD? P_k has rank? For k fixed, P_k is a permutation-like matrix with 1 on anti-diagonal (and 1/2 diagonal). Not convex for indefinite. But if a>=0, maybe P_k not needed.

Maybe use variables x_i = a_i^2? Then c_k = sum sqrt(x_i x_{k-i}). Concave in x? sqrt product concave? Product of square roots is concave on R_+^2? The geometric mean is concave. Sum concave, constraints c_k <= τ is concave <= τ, not convex (sublevel of concave is nonconvex). Hmm.

Could use logistic smoothing: let a_i = exp(b_i), sum exp=1. Objective log max c. c_k = sum exp(b_i+b_{k-i}). log c_k convex in b, max convex. Constraints sum exp=1 not convex? log-sum-exp(b)=0 is convex equality (nonconvex). But can use minimize log(max c)+λ(logsumexp(b)-0)^2? Not guaranteed.

Maybe use gradient flow in b with entropy regularization: minimize max c, b free, with normalization by softmax. If a=softmax(b), sum=1 automatically. Gradient of c_k wrt b_i = a_i * a_{k-i} (plus symmetric? Actually c_k=sum a_i a_{k-i}, with a=softmax(b), derivative ∂a_i/∂b_j = a_i(δ_ij - a_j). Then ∂c_k = sum_j a_j a_{k-j} ∂? Easier: derivative wrt a_i is 2a_{k-i}; chain to b_j: ∂c/∂b_j = sum_i ∂c/∂a_i ∂a_i/∂b_j = 2a_{k-j} a_j (1 - a_j). If objective max, gradient = active avg. This enforces sum=1 and positivity, maybe easier. Then update b -= lr * gradient (negative b to reduce a where gradient high). This is multiplicative updates.

This may converge nicely. Let's formulate:
For current a (sum 1), compute c and active set A (top few lags). Gradient in b:
h_j = average over k in A [ 2 a_j a_{k-j} (1 - a_j) for valid k-j ]
= 2 a_j (1-a_j) * q_j, where q_j = average_{k in A} a_{k-j} (valid).
To minimize F=max c, take b_j -= η h_j (since gradient of F wrt b). Equivalent a_j multiply by exp(-η h_j) then renormalize softmax. But softmax update with b shift: a_j_new = a_j e^{-η h_j} / Z.
This pushes down high-value positions that contribute to active lag, with factor (1-a_j) for near-convex. Could be stable.

Alternatively beamin-m focusing of active set: If only one active lag k*, new a_j ∝ old a_j * exp(-2η a_j a_{k*-j}(1-a_j))? For small η approximates multiplicative reduction. Could get stuck.

Could use Frank-Wolfe: define linear approximation of max c at active k*: min linear gradient over simplex => set mass on lowest gradient coordinate. But maybe too aggressive.

Maybe direct LP approach is more reliable. Let's investigate LP subproblem approximate but can be used as projected gradient in original nonconvex? Given f, solve min m s.t. f*y <= m, sum y=1. This finds y minimizing cross with f. This is analogous to finding best response in a 2-player zero-sum game? Could lead to equilibrium with low autocorr. But we can use a simple subgradient for cross-minimization: y update = multiplicative weights to reduce max f*y. Since f fixed, this is a convex problem (minimize max linear functions) and can be solved fast by multiplicative weights without LP:
Initialize y uniform. Repeat:
- compute s_k = (f*y)[k] via convolution (O(n log n))
- M=max s
- for each position i, it contributes to constraints k where weight f_{k-i}. To constrain linear functions, subgradient for y_i for active k is f_{k-i}. Project negative gradient with sum=1: h_i = avg active f_{k-i}; y_i ∝ y_i * exp(-η h_i). This is dual ascent for minimize max linear. After normalized, M' may reduce. This is much cheaper! Then use y as direction to update f.

So two-level algorithm:
- Outer: maintain f (probability simplex).
- Inner: compute y = best response to f by solving/approximating min_m max_k (f*y)_k. Use multiplicative weights with active f*y constraints.
- Update f ← (1-λ)f + λ y, line search on score.
This avoids LP.

What optimization method? Directly minimize F=max c. Let's implement a smooth/robust algorithm:
1. Normalize vector a to sum=1.
2. Compute c via FFT.
3. Use active set: all k with c_k > τ (e.g. max - 0.01 range). Average gradients.
4. Gradient wrt a_i for active k: g_i = (1/|A|) ∑_{k in A} 2 a_{|?|?} (valid). Actually ∂c_k/∂a_i = 2 a_{k-i} if valid else 0; since A active lags. So g_i = 2 * weighted average of a_{k-i} over active k valid.
5. Project onto simplex (sum=1,a>=0) for negative gradient direction: d = -g - (-g).mean()? To maintain sum, direction h = -g + mean(g) (because we subtract from a). Then line search: new a = a + step*h clipped/renormalized. But h may make negative; project properly.

Projection of a + step h onto simplex is O(n log n) if step scalar. But h has zeros? We can instead update multiplicatively in log domain with softmax to maintain positivity. However gradient in a might be noisy. Let's use log-space: b_i=log a_i (or b free, a=softmax(b) to avoid clipping). Gradient in b as derived: h_b_i = 2 a_i(1-a_i) q_i, where q_i = average_{active k} a_{k-i}. Then b -= lr*h_b. This enforces sum=1 and positivity. Complexity O(|A|n) if naive. But |A| small; can compute q via for each active k add shifted a. For active K up to maybe 100, O(K n) per iter. With FFT maybe faster? For K small, direct OK. n=4096,K=20 ->80k ops, many iters.

But if active set includes many lags (e.g. threshold close max with many near equal), K can be large. We can select top K by c values (K=20 or 50). This approximates subgradient.

Gradient for b: given c, w = c - maxc; w=exp(β w?) For logsumexp, L = logsumexp(c/τ). dL/db = (1/τ) ∑_k w_k ∂c_k/∂a_i * ∂a_i/∂b_j. In matrix form: grad_a_i = (2/τ) ∑_k w_k a_{k-i} (valid). grad_b_j = ∑_i grad_a_i a_j(δ_ij - a_j) = a_j (grad_a_j - ∑_i grad_a_i a_i). Let s = dot(grad_a, a). Then grad_b = a*(grad_a - s). Let's derive efficient: grad_a_i = (2/τ) ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a_j w_{i+j}, where w_k=softmax(c/τ) for k=0..2n-2. (With c/τ). We can compute via FFT: grad_a_i = 2/τ * (a * w reversed?) Let Wrev? If we define B_m = w_{m}. The output h_i = ∑_j a_j B_{i+j}. This is convolution of a with reversed B: Let C = a * B where (a*B)_k=∑_j a_j B_{k-j}. Set k = ? Need i+j = k+j? Function local_optimize(a, time_budget, seed):
- a normalized sum1.

Here h_i = gradient wrt b for max (top K) = 2 a_i q_i (1? if using exact gradient of F wrt b? Wait if we want decrease F, use gradient of F wrt b. For max over active A, F=1/|A|∑_{k∈A} c_k. ∂F/∂b_j = 2 a_j(1-a_j) q_j, where q_j=avg_{k∈A} a_{k-j}. Then update b -= lr * grad, a scales by exp(-lr grad). This stays positive/normalized.
- q_j computation: for each active k, for j valid (0≤k-j<n), q_j += a_{k-j}. Then q averaged.
- Line search: try lr in sequence (e.g. 0.5,0.25,0.1,...). For each, a_trial = a * exp(-lr*grad); normalize; compute Ftrial. Accept if Ftrial < best_F (or < current F). If none, reduce K or add random perturbation.
- This is a gradient descent in b; exact objective but active set approximate. It may converge.
- To avoid flat directions, if no improvement for many iters, apply random perturbation in b (scale), or random coordinate shake, or use second-order? We can also run "Hessian-free" with random directions?

Local optimizer with log update:
a = softmax(b). Compute c = fftconvolve(a,a). F=max c. Active lags top K. Compute q_i = avg_{active k} a_{k-i} (valid). grad_b_i = 2 a_i(1-a_i) q_i. Since a_i ~1/n, q_i~1/n, grad ~2/n^2. Step size should be O(n^2). We can adapt line search: scale gradient to have e.g. max abs =1, use step size parameter α. b_new = b - α * (grad / G). Since softmax invariant to adding constant, we can center grad to avoid drift: grad -= mean(grad). Then multiply by some scale. Let s = norm1? Use step η; we can test η in [1,2,0.5,...] with normalized grad.

But gradient wrt b for objective F. If we center grad, softmax update unchanged by constant. We can set direction d_i = -grad_i + mean(grad). Then trial b = b + step*(d/dmax). dmax ~2/n^2. step maybe 1e3? Instead use multiplicative update directly: a_trial = a * exp(-lr * h) where h_i = q_i (or q scaled). For small lr*h, sum change. Since h~1/n, lr can be O(n). We can line search over lr in [n*0.1, ...]. Or use a known formula: a_i ∝ a_i exp(-η q_i). q_i is the "force" reducing high products. We can choose η=0.1*n maybe.

Better use simulated annealing / multiplicative weights for high F lags:
For each iteration:
- compute c, find top K lags.
- For each top lag k, it contributes weight w. For each i, multiplier m[i] *= (second factor?) Maybe the subgradient in a is g_i = avg 2a_{k-i}. Multiplicative update: a_i <- a_i * exp(-η g_i / (avg a (1-a)?)). In log form use grad_b.

Let's implement log update with line search. Pseudocode:
```
def optimize_vec(a0, max_time, seed):
    a0 = normalize clip positive
    b0 = log(a0 + 1e-300)
    b = b0.copy()
    a = softmax(b)
    best_score = score_from_a(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    it=0
    stall=0
    eta = 100.0 # in units of b?
    while time:
        c = conv(a)
        F = c.max()
        if F < best_score: best...
        # active top K
        K = 32
        if len(c)>K: idx = argpartition(c,K)[-K:]
        else idx all
        # q = zeros
        q = np.zeros(n)
        for k in idx:
            lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(k,n-1)
            q[lo:hi+1] += a[k - np.arange(lo,hi+1)]
        q /= len(idx)  # maybe unnormalized
        # grad b = 2a(1-a) q; center it
        gb = 2*a*(1-a)*q
        gb = gb - gb.mean()
        scale = np.max(np.abs(gb))
        if scale==0: break
        gb_norm = gb/scale
        accepted=False
        for step in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125,0.015625,0.0078125]:
            b_t = b - step * gb_norm * scale_factor? 
```
Here gb_norm dimensionless step 1 may be too large (changes b by 1, probabilities huge). Step in b should be small. If we use gb normalized, step maybe 0.01. But the correct gradient magnitude relates to eta. We can instead use trial update a_t = a * exp(-lr * q), with lr variable. q~1/n, to change probabilities by O(1) lr~n. Let's use that: q positive; lr candidates in [0.01,0.05,0.1,0.5,1] * n? For n=4096, lr*n? Actually a*exp(-lr*q). If lr=1, q~1/n, log change ~-1/n, score change second order tiny. def smooth_objective_and_grad(z, tau, penalty):
    a = softmax(z)
    # make N_fft for correlate
    L=2*n-1
    Nfft = 1 << (3*n-2).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, Nfft)
    # p = softmax(c/tau)
    c = scipy.signal.fftconvolve(a,a) # real length L
    # or use fa^2 ifft: c = ifft(fa*fa)[:L].real (faster)

def make_a(z):
    z = z - z.max()
    e = np.exp(z)
    return e / e.sum()

Exact active-set polish (optional):
```
def polish(a, time_budget):
    a = normalize positive
    best=a.copy(); bestF=max(conv(a))
    K=16
    eta_scale = n
    while time:
       c=conv(a); F=max(c)
       if F<bestF: best...
       idx=top K
       q=zeros
       for k in idx: q[lo:hi+1]+=a[k - arange]
       q/=len(idx)
       # try multiplicative updates in log: b=log(a+eps) - lr*(q - q.mean())
       b=np.log(a+1e-300)
       accepted=False
       for lr in [0.01,0.05,0.1,0.5,1,5] * maybe:
           b_t = b - lr*(q - q.mean())
           a_t=softmax(b_t)
           F_t=max(conv(a_t))
           if F_t < F - 1e-15: accept; break
       if not accept: maybe random perturb
    return best
```

def fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray; S=a.sum(); if S<0.01 return inf
    c=fft_convolve_full(a,a) # using fft
    return 2*len(a)*c.max()/(S*S)

Implementation convolve_fft:
```
def full_conv(a):
    n=len(a); N=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1].real
    return np.maximum(c,0.0)
```

In `fun_and_grad`:
```
n=STATE_N; NFFT=STATE_NFFT; L=2*n-1
z = np.asarray(z)
a = softmax(z)
fa = np.fft.fft(a, NFFT)
c = np.fft.ifft(fa*fa, NFFT)[:L].real
c = np.clip(c,0,None)
m = c.max()
exp = np.exp((c-m)/STATE_TAU)
sumexp = exp.sum()
p = exp/sumexp
h = np.fft.ifft(np.fft.fft(p,NFFT)*np.conj(fa), NFFT)[:n].real
# Due circular no wrap because NFFT>=L+n-1
grad_a = 2*h
s = np.dot(grad_a, a)
grad_z = a*(grad_a - s)
if STATE_GAMMA>0: grad_z = grad_z + STATE_GAMMA*z
val = (m + STATE_TAU*np.log(sumexp))
if STATE_GAMMA>0: val += 0.5*STATE_GAMMA*np.dot(z,z)
return val, grad_z
```

Active-set polish details:
```
def polish_sequence(a_in, deadline):
    a = np.clip(a,0,None); a/=a.sum(); best=a.copy(); bestF=fast_maxc(a)
    K=24
    b = np.log(a+1e-300) # maintain log
    # We can directly update b using q = top avg; maybe scale
    base = n
    steps = [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0,10.0] # multiply by base? 
    no=0
    while time<deadline:
       # ensure b from a? If b not updated after acceptance, better maintain b
       # Use b as state; a=softmax(b)
       c=conv(a); F=c.max()
       if F<bestF: best...
       idx=top K
       q=zeros; for k: q[lo:hi]+=a[k - idx]
       q/=len(idx)
       q = q - q.mean()
       # scale: q ~1/n; step*base change O(step)
       accepted=False
       for s in [0.001,0.003,0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0,10.0]:
           step = s * n #? q*n has O1? Let's compute q*n ~1. step=s*n * q? If we do b_t = b - s*(q*n - q*n.mean()), change O(s). Then s from 0.001 to1.
           b_t = b - s * (q*n - q.mean()*n)  # = b - s*n*(q - mean)
           b_t = np.clip(b_t, BMIN,BMAX)
           a_t=softmax(b_t)
           F_t=fast_maxc(a_t)
           if F_t < F*(1-1e-12) - 1e-15:
               b=b_t; a=a_t; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           # local random: perturb b Gaussian small
           for scale in [0.05,0.2,0.5]:
              b_t = b + scale*rng.standard_normal(n)
              a_t=softmax(clip)
              if fast_maxc(a_t)<F: accept
           else:
              break or reduce K/steps
       print every 100
    return best
```

Start generation details:
```
def make_starts(N, rng, global_seq):
    starts=[]
    def add(z, name): z=...; if finite: starts.append((name,z))
    # global
    if global_seq: interpolate
    # block ratios [1.5,2,3,4]
    for ratio in [1.5,2,3,4]: logd=np.log(ratio); z=zeros; z[:N//2]=-logd/2; z[N//2:]=logd/2
    # linear A [0.2,0.5,0.8,1.1] f=1+A(u-0.5) clipped >0; z=log f
    for A in [0.2,0.5,0.8,1.1]: f=1+A*(u-0.5); f=np.maximum(f,1e-6); z=np.log(f); center
    # quadratic A [0.3,0.6,1.0]
    for A in [0.3,0.6,1.0]: f=1+A*( (2u-1)**2 -1/3); clip log
    # trig: A*cos(2πu), A*sin(2πu), A*cos(4πu)
    for A in [0.3,0.6,0.9]: add A*np.cos(2πu), etc.
    # random smooth: 2 starts
    for _ in range(2): z = sum coeff * cos/sin modes 1..5, coeff normal*0.3; add
```

Let's detail `polish`:
```
def polish_sequence(a_in, deadline, K=32):
    n=len(a_in); a=... normalize
    b=np.log(a+1e-300)
    # clip b within [-30,30]
    best=a.copy(); bestF=conv_max(a)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(123)
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       F=conv_max(a)
       if F<bestF-best tolerance: best=a; bestF=F; no_improve=0
       elif no_improve>100: maybe random perturb and reset? 
       c=conv_full? We need c for active idx; compute conv_full(a)
       F=c.max()
       # top K
       if len(c)>K:
           idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
       else idx=np.arange(len(c))
       # Optional: choose weights sorted descending maybe all equal
       q=np.zeros(n)
       for k in idx:
           lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(k,n-1)
           if hi>=lo: q[lo:hi+1] += a[k - np.arange(lo,hi+1)]
       q /= len(idx)
       # Update b by -eta*(q - mean q). Use eta candidates in b units: q*n has O1.
       centered = (q - q.mean())*n
       # Limit centered to avoid huge
       centered = np.clip(centered, -5,5)
       accepted=False
       for eta in [0.001,0.003,0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0,10.0]:
           b_t = b - eta*centered
           if eta>0: b_t = np.clip(b_t, -30,30)
           a_t = softmax(b_t)
           F_t=conv_max(a_t)
           if F_t < F - 1e-15:
               b=b_t; a=a_t; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           # Try random smooth perturbations: b_t=b+scale*rng.normal; maybe low-pass.
           accepted2=False
           for scale in [0.01,0.05,0.2,0.5]:
              b_t=np.clip(b + scale*rng.standard_normal(n), -30,30)
              a_t=softmax(b_t); F_t=conv_max(a_t)
              if F_t < bestF: # accept if improves best
                  b=b_t; a=a_t; accepted2=True; break
           if accepted2: no_improve=0
           else:
              no_improve +=1
              if no_improve > 200: break
       if it%50 ==0 print
    return best
```

Potential issue: Using full `np.fft.fft` of real a length 32768 in objective and p; we can use `rfft`/`irfft` for speed. Let's derive safe rfft correlation for h:
For real arrays x (length L) and y (length n), cross-correlation z[k]=∑_{j=0}^{n-1} x[k+j] y[j] for k=0..L-n. The DFT theorem: z = ifft(fft(x) * conj(fft(y)))[:L-n+1]. With rfft, we can compute X=np.fft.rfft(x,N), Y=np.fft.rfft(y,N). The product Z = X * conj(Y) is not conjugate-symmetric? Since X and Y are rfft of real, X[k]=conj(X[N-k]), Y similarly. Product P[k]=X[k]conj(Y[k]). P[N-k]=X[N-k]conj(Y[N-k]) = conj(X[k]) conj(conj(Y[k])) = conj(X[k])Y[k] = conj(P[k])? because scalar commute: conj(P[k])=conj(X[k]) Y[k]. Yes P is conjugate symmetric. So irfft can recover real circular correlation. Great. Use rfft for both. For c=conv(a,a), c = irfft(fa*fa) (fa real rfft, product not conjugate symmetric? fa*fa not symmetric? For convolution, FFT of real convolution is F*F, which is conjugate symmetric because F[N-k]=conj(F[k]), F[N-k]*F[N-k]=conj(F[k])*conj(F[k])=conj(F[k]*F[k]), yes). So can use rfft for c too.
Thus objective:
```
fa = np.fft.rfft(a, NFFT)
c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, NFFT)[:L].real
fp = np.fft.rfft(p, NFFT)
h = np.fft.irfft(fp * np.conj(fa), NFFT)[:n].real
```
This is faster.

def _set_opt(n, tau):
    global _OPT_N, _OPT_NFFT, _OPT_TAU
    _OPT_N=n; _OPT_NFFT=1 << (3*n-2).bit_length(); _OPT_TAU=tau

def _run_minimize(z0, maxfun, maxiter):
   opts = {'maxfun':maxfun,'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12,'pgtol':1e-10,'disp':False}
   res=minimize(_obj_grad, z0, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=[(-_OPT_BOUND,_OPT_BOUND)]* _OPT_N, options=opts)
   return res
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42,budget_s=2400,**kwargs):
    budget=max(60,float(budget_s))
    deadline = time.monotonic()+budget-5
    N=8192 if budget>=1500 else 4096
    _set_opt(N, 1e-6)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    starts=_make_starts(rng)
    # fallback if none
    block_z = ...
    filtered top 12
    best_a=None; best_score=np.inf
    top_candidates=[]
    for i,(sc,z) in enumerate(starts):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 300: break
       if i>=12: break
       maxfun=700 if i<4 else 350
       maxiter=200 if i<4 else 120
       try: res=_run_minimize(z, maxfun, maxiter)
       except: continue
       if res is None: continue
       a=_softmax(np.clip(res.x,...)); sc2=_fast_score(a)
       if finite and sc2<best_score: best...
       # add to top candidates if sc2 finite
       top_candidates.append((sc2,a))
       top_candidates.sort(key=_score_key)
       top_candidates=top_candidates[:3]
       print
    if best_a is None:
       best_a = _block_sequence(N); best_score=_fast_score(best_a)
    # polish top candidates
    if top_candidates:
       # polish best first, maybe second if time
       for idx,(sc,a) in enumerate(top_candidates):
          time_left=deadline-time.monotonic()
          if time_left<60: break
          # allocate: for idx0, up to 70% remaining; for idx1, 30%?
          if idx==0:
             budget_pol = min(time_left-30, 1200)
          else:

          if budget_pol<=0: break
          a2=_polish(a, time.monotonic()+budget_pol)
          sc2=_fast_score(a2)
          if finite and sc2<best_score: best...
    # final optional call evaluate_sequence?
    try:
       exact=evaluate_sequence(best_a.tolist())
       if np.isfinite(exact):
           print...
    except: pass
    # Ensure positive, bounded
    best_a=np.clip(np.asarray(best_a,float),0.0,1000.0)
    S=best_a.sum()
    if S<0.01: best_a=np.ones(N)/N
    else: best_a=best_a/S
    return [float(x) for x in best_a]

Do not use lambda in `top_candidates.sort`; define key.

Need if `starts` empty: fallback. In `_make_starts`, if global sequence unavailable, still adds synthetic. So not empty.

Let's include in `_make_starts` limit: if `starts` > 30, sort and top 30. In propose top 10.

Now, final code. We'll produce only code fence.

Double-check variable names: `_OPT_NFFT = 1 << (3*n - 2).bit_length()` If n=0 not. n>=1. For n=8192, 3n-2=24574, bit_length 15, 1<<15=32768. Good.

Need `min` and `max` builtins okay.

Let's write final in final answer.
</think>

```python
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize

"""
Algorithm summary
-----------------
We search for a non-negative probability vector a (sum(a)=1) minimizing

    score(a) = 2*n*max_k (a*a)[k],

where * is linear convolution.  The search is done in log-space with
a_i = softmax(z)_i, using a smooth approximation to the maximum
autocorrelation value:

    F_tau(a) = tau * logsumexp(c/tau),  c = a*a.

This gives an efficient full-vector gradient.  We run L-BFGS-B from
many structured starts (block, linear, smooth step, sinusoidal, and the
provided global sequence when available), then polish with an exact
active-set gradient line search in log-space.
"""

_OPT_N = 0
_OPT_NFFT = 0
_OPT_TAU = 1e-6
_OPT_BOUND = 30.0
_OPT_BOUNDS = []


def _set_opt_state(n, tau=1e-6):
    global _OPT_N, _OPT_NFFT, _OPT_TAU, _OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUNDS
    _OPT_N = int(n)
    _OPT_NFFT = 1 << (3 * _OPT_N - 2).bit_length()
    _OPT_TAU = float(tau)
    _OPT_BOUND = 30.0
    _OPT_BOUNDS = [(-_OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUND)] * _OPT_N


def _softmax(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if z.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    z = z - np.max(z)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full_like(z, 1.0 / z.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def _fft_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = _OPT_N
    N = _OPT_NFFT
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if a.size != n:
        n = a.size
        N = 1 << (3 * n - 2).bit_length()
        L = 2 * n - 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L].real
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    c = _fft_conv(a)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2 * a.size * m / (s * s))


def _score_key(item):
    return item[0]


def _obj_grad(z):
    n = _OPT_N
    N = _OPT_NFFT
    tau = _OPT_TAU
    L = 2 * n - 1

    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if z.size != n:
        return 1e12, np.zeros(max(1, z.size), dtype=np.float64)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        z = np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=_OPT_BOUND, neginf=-_OPT_BOUND)
    z = np.clip(z, -_OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUND)

    a = _softmax(z)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L].real
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)

    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return 1e12, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    diff = (c - m) / tau
    diff = np.clip(diff, -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(diff)
    se = float(np.sum(e))
    if se <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(se):
        return 1e12, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    p = e / se
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    h = np.fft.irfft(fp * np.conjugate(fa), N)[:n].real

    # dF/dz = 2 * a * (h - sum(a*h)), with F = tau*logsumexp(c/tau)
    s_h = float(np.dot(h, a))
    g = a * (h - s_h)
    grad = 2.0 * g

    val = m + tau * np.log(se)

    # Scale objective/gradient to a numerically comfortable range.
    scale = float(n) * float(n)
    val = val * scale
    grad = grad * scale

    # Tiny penalty to remove the exact softmax gauge ambiguity.
    pen = 1e-10
    val += 0.5 * pen * float(np.dot(z, z))
    grad = grad + pen * z

    if not np.isfinite(val):
        val = 1e12
        grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    return float(val), grad


def _run_l_bfgs(z0, maxfun=500, maxiter=150):
    z0 = np.asarray(z0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if z0.size != _OPT_N:
        return None
    z0 = np.clip(z0 - np.mean(z0), -_OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUND)

    opts = {
        "maxfun": int(maxfun),
        "maxiter": int(maxiter),
        "ftol": 1e-12,
        "pgtol": 1e-10,
        "disp": False,
    }
    try:
        res = minimize(
            _obj_grad,
            z0,
            jac=True,
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            bounds=_OPT_BOUNDS,
            options=opts,
        )
        return res
    except Exception:
        return None


def _z_from_f(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if f.size != _OPT_N:
        return None
    f = np.where(np.isfinite(f), f, 1.0)
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-8)
    z = np.log(f)
    z = z - float(np.mean(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -_OPT_BOUND + 1.0, _OPT_BOUND - 1.0)
    return z


def _block_sequence(n):
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    half = n // 2
    a[:half] = 1.0
    a[half:] = 2.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0:
        a[:] = 1.0 / n
    else:
        a /= s
    return a


def _add_start(starts, z, score_cache=None):
    if z is None:
        return
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if z.size != _OPT_N or not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        return
    z = np.clip(z - np.mean(z), -_OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUND)
    a = _softmax(z)
    sc = _fast_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        starts.append((float(sc), z))


def _make_starts(rng):
    n = _OPT_N
    starts = []

    u = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=True)
    x = 2.0 * u - 1.0

    # Provided sequence, resampled to current length, if available.
    try:
        g = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
        if g is not None:
            g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
            if g.size > 1:
                g = np.clip(g, 1e-300, 1000.0)
                if np.all(np.isfinite(g)) and float(np.sum(g)) > 0.0:
                    z_old = np.log(g)
                    z_old = z_old - float(np.mean(z_old))
                    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, g.size, endpoint=True)
                    z = np.interp(np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=True), x_old, z_old)
                    _add_start(starts, z)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Simple two-level blocks.
    half = n // 2
    for ratio in (1.2, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5, 3.0, 4.0, 6.0):
        f = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        f[:half] = 1.0
        f[half:] = float(ratio)
        _add_start(starts, _z_from_f(f))

    # Monotone linear profiles f = 1 + A*x.
    for A in (0.15, 0.30, 0.45, 0.60, 0.75, 0.90, 1.10, 1.35):
        f = 1.0 + A * x
        _add_start(starts, _z_from_f(f))

    # Smooth monotone steps.
    for A in (0.5, 0.75, 0.95):
        for w in (0.02, 0.04, 0.08, 0.15, 0.25, 0.40, 0.60):
            sig = 4.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-(x / max(1e-3, w)))) - 1.0
            f = 1.0 + A * sig
            _add_start(starts, _z_from_f(f))

    # Quadratic and trigonometric perturbations.
    for A in (0.25, 0.5, 0.8, 1.2, 1.6):
        f = 1.0 + A * (x * x - 1.0 / 3.0)
        _add_start(starts, _z_from_f(f))

    for A in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75):
        for m in (1, 2, 3):
            f = 1.0 + A * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * u)
            _add_start(starts, _z_from_f(f))
            f = 1.0 + A * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * u)
            _add_start(starts, _z_from_f(f))

    # Random smooth starts.
    for _ in range(6):
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in range(1, 7):
            c1 = 0.18 * rng.standard_normal()
            c2 = 0.18 * rng.standard_normal()
            f += c1 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * u)
            f += c2 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * u)
        _add_start(starts, _z_from_f(f))

    if len(starts) > 40:
        starts.sort(key=_score_key)
        starts = starts[:40]
    else:
        starts.sort(key=_score_key)

    return starts


def _polish(a0, deadline):
    n = _OPT_N
    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    if a.size != n:
        return a0

    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return _block_sequence(n)
    a /= s

    b = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    b = np.clip(b - np.mean(b), -_OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUND)

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = _fast_score(a)
    best_maxc = best_score / (2.0 * n)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(987654321)
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = _fft_conv(a)
        F = float(np.max(c))

        if F < best_maxc - 1e-18:
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = 2.0 * n * F
            best_maxc = F

        m = F
        diff = (c - m) / _OPT_TAU
        diff = np.clip(diff, -745.0, 0.0)
        e = np.exp(diff)
        se = float(np.sum(e))
        if se <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(se):
            break
        p = e / se

        N = _OPT_NFFT
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
        fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
        h = np.fft.irfft(fp * np.conjugate(fa), N)[:n].real
        s_h = float(np.dot(h, a))
        g = a * (h - s_h)

        # Same scaling as used in the L-BFGS objective.
        g = g * (float(n) * float(n))
        g = np.clip(g, -10.0, 10.0)

        accepted = False
        for eta in (1e-4, 3e-4, 1e-3, 3e-3, 1e-2, 3e-2, 1e-1, 3e-1, 1.0, 3.0, 10.0):
            b_t = np.clip(b - eta * g, -_OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUND)
            a_t = _softmax(b_t)
            F_t = float(np.max(_fft_conv(a_t)))
            if F_t < F - 1e-15:
                b = b_t
                a = a_t
                accepted = True
                break

        if not accepted:
            # Random escape in log-space, accepted only if it beats the global best so far.
            escaped = False
            for scale in (0.005, 0.02, 0.08, 0.25, 0.75):
                b_t = np.clip(b + scale * rng.standard_normal(n), -_OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUND)
                a_t = _softmax(b_t)
                F_t = float(np.max(_fft_conv(a_t)))
                if F_t < best_maxc - 1e-15:
                    b = b_t
                    a = a_t
                    escaped = True
                    break
            if escaped:
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve += 1
                if no_improve > 200:
                    break
        else:
            no_improve = 0

        it += 1
        if it % 50 == 0:
            cur_score = 2.0 * n * F
            print(f"polish it={it} current={cur_score:.9f} best={best_score:.9f} "
                  f"time_left={max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic()):.0f}s")

    return best


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search by log-L-BFGS from many structured starts, then exact polish.
    Returns the best non-negative coefficient list found.
    """
    budget_s = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + budget_s - 6.0

    # Use a fairly large length; 8192 is still fast with FFT and gives a
    # good finite-length empirical score.
    n = 8192 if budget_s >= 1300.0 else 4096
    _set_opt_state(n, tau=1e-6)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t0 = time.monotonic()

    starts = _make_starts(rng)
    if not starts:
        starts = [(2.0, None)]

    best_a = _block_sequence(n)
    best_score = _fast_score(best_a)
    print(f"initial fallback score={best_score:.9f}, n={n}")

    candidates = []

    # Process the most promising starts first.  Use more iterations for the
    # very best starts, but keep overall time bounded by maxfun.
    for idx, (start_score, z0) in enumerate(starts[:14]):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 240.0:
            break

        if idx < 5:
            maxfun = 900
            maxiter = 260
        elif idx < 10:
            maxfun = 550
            maxiter = 170
        else:
            maxfun = 320
            maxiter = 110

        res = _run_l_bfgs(z0, maxfun=maxfun, maxiter=maxiter)
        if res is not None and hasattr(res, "x"):
            z_new = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if z_new.size == n:
                a = _softmax(np.clip(z_new, -_OPT_BOUND, _OPT_BOUND))
                sc = _fast_score(a)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    if sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc
                        best_a = a.copy()
                        print(f"L-BFGS start {idx}: score={sc:.9f} "
                              f"elapsed={time.monotonic() - t0:.0f}s")
                    candidates.append((sc, a.copy()))
                    if len(candidates) > 5:
                        candidates.sort(key=_score_key)
                        candidates = candidates[:5]

    # Final polishing on the best candidates.  The most intensive polish is
    # given to the current best, with smaller attempts on high-quality
    # alternatives if time remains.
    if candidates:
        candidates.sort(key=_score_key)
        unique_cands = []
        seen = set()
        for sc, a in candidates:
            key = tuple(np.round(a, 10).tolist())
            if key not in seen:
                seen.add(key)
                unique_cands.append((sc, a))
        candidates = unique_cands[:4]

        for ci, (sc, a) in enumerate(candidates):
            time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if time_left < 45.0:
                break

            if ci == 0:
                pol_deadline = time.monotonic() + min(max(20.0, time_left - 20.0), 1500.0)
            else:
                pol_deadline = time.monotonic() + min(max(15.0, time_left / 3.0), 360.0)

            try:
                a2 = _polish(a, pol_deadline)
                sc2 = _fast_score(a2)
                if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
                    best_score = sc2
                    best_a = np.asarray(a2, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                    print(f"polished candidate {ci}: score={sc2:.9f} "
                          f"elapsed={time.monotonic() - t0:.0f}s")
            except Exception:
                pass

    # Safety: validate against the official evaluator if available.
    try:
        best_a = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        best_a = np.nan_to_num(best_a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        best_a = np.clip(best_a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(np.sum(best_a))
        if s < 0.01 or not np.isfinite(s):
            best_a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
        else:
            best_a = best_a / s

        exact = evaluate_sequence(best_a.tolist())
        if np.isfinite(exact):
            print(f"official evaluator score={float(exact):.9f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Final defensive normalization.
    best_a = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    best_a = np.nan_to_num(best_a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    best_a = np.clip(best_a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(best_a))
    if s < 0.01 or not np.isfinite(s):
        best_a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    else:
        best_a = best_a / s

    return [float(v) for v in best_a.tolist()]
```
